[CHARACTER CREATION - CONCEPT]

Welcome, Aegon. Your blood carries the heat of old Valyria, though it has been cooled by the harsh life of a sellsword. At eighteen, you are a shadow-walker, a Gloomstalker who thrives in the dark—yet your claim to the Iron Throne is a secret that could either set the world on fire or get you silenced forever.

To prove yourself to Ser Gregor Clegane, 'The Mountain,' is to embrace the abyss. He doesn't care for lineage; he cares for the efficiency of slaughter and the silence of the dead. Your unique path allows you to use your Wisdom—your keen insight into the nature of men and the world—to persuade even the most depraved souls.

Before we begin your audition for the Mountain's men, we must finalize your mechanical foundation. I have prepared a template based on your request.

SCENE 7
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:15:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Lvl 5 Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6500/14000 | Gold: 125gp]
Resources: HD: 5/5 | Spells: L1 4/4, L2 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20+7 = 25 vs DC 12 - Success (Perception (Observe Camp))

Game Master:
Morning (08:15:00) in the Riverlands, near Mummer's Ford—the sky is the color of a fresh bruise, purples and deep greys hanging low over the smoldering remains of a nameless hamlet. The air is thick, cloying with the coppery tang of blood and the greasy stench of wet woodsmoke. It is a familiar incense to those who serve the sword, and to Aegon, it is the smell of opportunity.

You stand at the edge of a muddy track, your ethereal features hooded against the damp morning chill. Your gaze, violet-tinged and unsettlingly beautiful, drifts over the scene of butchery. A dozen men in the yellow tabards of House Clegane are systematically dismantling what remains of the village's dignity. A woman’s scream, sharp and jagged, is cut short by the wet thud of steel on bone. One of the men, a brute with a face like a slab of raw meat, laughs as he tosses a bloodied silk ribbon into a growing pile of loot.

In the center of the chaos sits Ser Gregor Clegane. Even seated on his massive destrier, he looms like a mountain of dark iron. His plate is scorched, caked in the gore of the morning’s work. He does not speak; he simply watches, his presence a heavy, suffocating weight that keeps even his most savage men in a state of trembling obedience. They are the Mountain's Men—Raff the Sweet, Chiswyck, the Tickler—monsters in human skin, and you have come to join their ranks.

You have shadowed them for three leagues, unseen in the gloom of the treeline. Now, as the fires die down to glowing embers and the 'fun' transitions to the labor of enslavement, it is time to emerge. You feel that familiar thrill in your gut—the sadistic spark that flares whenever you witness the exquisite fragility of the weak. They have the power, but you have the shadow.

Game Master:
You remain crouched in the tall, wet grass at the edge of the smoldering village, your violet-tinged eyes fixed on the massive silhouette of Ser Gregor. The air is still, save for the crackle of burning thatch and the distant, rhythmic sobbing of a survivor. You feel the cold weight of your shortswords against your thighs, the steel a comfort in this place of slaughter. (Wisdom Check: 21 vs DC 14 — Success) Your mind works with a sharp, predatory clarity, dissecting the scene before you. These men aren't soldiers; they are dogs on a very short leash, and they only understand one thing: the hierarchy of the kill. Your sharp instincts (WIS 16) suggest that an approach based on shared depravity and undeniable utility will serve you better than any claim of high birth or empty promises. You have watched them long enough to know where the cracks in their discipline lie.

SCENE 12
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:20:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Lvl 5 Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6500/14000 | Gold: 125gp]
Resources: HD: 5/5, Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2, Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20+6 = 24 vs DC 18 - Success (Persuasion (Wisdom-based))

Game Master:
Morning (08:20:00) in the Riverlands, near Mummer's Ford—the mist is thick enough to swallow a man whole, but it parts like a curtain as you step onto the muddy road. You weave the threads of the Weave with practiced ease, your ethereal, unsettling beauty shifting into the grime-streaked, hollowed-eyed visage of a sellsword who has seen too many miles and too little coin. Your boots crunch on the charred remains of a fence as you drag the Riverrun scout's head by its matted hair, the blood leaving a dark, stuttering trail in the muck.

The yellow-clad outriders of House Clegane are on you in an instant, steel rasping from scabbards. Raff the Sweet levels a spear at your chest, a cruel grin splitting his face. "Look what the smoke coughed up," he sneers. You ignore him, walking with a steady, purposeful gait toward the towering shadow of Ser Gregor Clegane. Ten feet from his destrier, you stop and hurl the head. It wetly thumps at the giant's stirrup, rolling once to reveal the Tully trout embroidered on the man's dying tabard.

"He was watching your flank from the ridge," you say, your voice raspy and devoid of heat. "I don't like being followed, and I imagine a man of your stature likes it even less." 

The Mountain does not move. The only sound is the heavy, wet breathing of his horse and the crackle of a nearby beam giving way. Gregor’s eyes, hidden behind the slit of his greathelm, fix on you with the weight of a falling siege engine. The silence stretches, agonizingly long, until finally, a low rumble of a voice vibrates from the iron. "A scout," Clegane grunts, his gaze shifting to the head. "You have a name, sellsword?"

Player (choice: sadistic_suggestion):
Offer Interrogation Help - The Tickler is nearby with a survivor; use your Insight to suggest a more visceral way to extract information, proving your shared sadism.

SCENE 13
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:25:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Lvl 5 Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6650/14000 | Gold: 125gp
Conditions: Disguised (Sellsword) | Inspiration: No]
Resources: HD: 5/5 | Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:
  - 2d20kh1+6 = 23 vs DC 15 - Success (Insight (Sadistic Advantage))
  - 1d20+6 = 11 vs DC 18 - Failure (Persuasion (Wisdom-based))

Game Master:
Morning (08:25:00) in the Riverlands, near Mummer's Ford—the air is thick with the metallic tang of blood and the low, rhythmic moaning of a man bound to a charred fence post nearby. Ser Gregor’s shadow stretches long across the mud, a dark monolith of iron that seems to suck the light from the grey sky. 

"Pale," you answer, the name sliding out like a blade from a felt-lined scabbard. You don't look at the Mountain; instead, your violet-tinged eyes drift toward the man in the leather jerkin—the Tickler—who is currently heating a short, blunt piece of iron in a bed of dying embers. The survivor tied to the post is a muddy mess of a man, his fingernails already missing, his eyes wide with a terror that has moved past screaming and into a wet, frantic whimpering.

You step closer to the torture site, your gaze cataloging the man's physical state with a cold, clinical hunger. You see the way he flinches at the sound of the iron scraping the coals, the specific way his breath hitches when the Tickler moves toward his left side. (Insight: 23 vs DC 15 - Success) "He's already expecting the iron," you say, your voice cutting through the survivor's whimpering. You look at the Tickler, offering a thin, predatory smile that holds no warmth. "Fire is for the impatient. Look at the way he guards his right side—the ribs are already cracked, likely from Raff's boot. If you use a slow, steady pressure there, just beneath the ninth rib, you can feel the lung begin to collapse without puncturing it. He'll feel the drown before he feels the heat. It keeps them conscious longer when the air starts to run thin."

The Tickler pauses, his salt-and-pepper hair matted with sweat. He looks from you to his 'project,' then back again, his expression unreadable. Ser Gregor’s horse shifts, its iron-shod hoof splashing in a puddle of gore. (Persuasion: 11 vs DC 18 - Failure) The Tickler snorts, a wet, phlegmy sound. "Think you're a scholar of the flesh, do you, Pale?" He turns back to the prisoner, ignoring your suggestion as he thrusts the glowing iron toward the man’s thigh. "I've got my own ways. Gregor didn't hire you to teach me my trade."

The Mountain’s rumble of a voice vibrates the very air. "The head was a start, Pale. But the Tickler works alone. If you want a place here, you'll find it in the muck with the rest of the dogs until I find a use for you."

[SOCIAL SKILL CHALLENGE: Ser Gregor Clegane]
Objective: Join the Mountain's Men
Social HP: 6/8 | Status: RESISTING
Resistance: "Find a place in the muck with the rest of the dogs."

SCENE 15
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:35:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Lvl 5 Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6650/14000 | Gold: 125gp
Conditions: Disguise Self (Active) | Exhaustion: 0 | Inspiration: No]
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20+0 = 19 vs DC 14 - Success (investigation)

Game Master:
Morning (08:35:00) in the smoldering ruins of the nameless hamlet near Mummer's Ford—the mist has begun to lift, replaced by a low, suffocating blanket of woodsmoke that tastes of wet thatch and rendered fat. You descend into the muck as Ser Gregor commanded, moving with the systematic coldness of a butcher. The Mountain's Men do not simply pillage; they strip the land bare, leaving nothing but scorched earth and the hollow eyes of those deemed too weak to even serve as slaves.

You find yourself working alongside Raff the Sweet and a few other sellswords, kicking through the wreckage of a weaver's cottage. Your violet-tinged eyes—hidden beneath the hollowed visage of your magical disguise—scan the debris with predatory efficiency. While Raff is content to smash pottery and pull brass rings from the ears of the dead, you look for the subtle signs of hidden value. (Investigation: 19 vs DC 14 - Success) You notice a loose floorboard beneath a blood-spattered loom. Prying it back with your crowbar reveals a small, iron-bound chest and a hidden crawlspace where a terrified boy, no more than twelve, huddles in the dark.

You reach into the crawlspace, your fingers closing around the boy's collar with bruising force. He whimpers, a sound that triggers a sharp, satisfying flare of sadism in your chest. You drag him out into the light, tossing him toward the center of the muddy track where the 'stock' is being gathered. The iron-bound chest yields a handful of silver stags and a heavy, tarnished brooch—small pickings, but proof of your thoroughness. Ser Gregor watches the proceedings from his massive horse, his gaze a silent, heavy judgment. You ensure he sees the way you handle the boy—no hesitation, no pity, just the cold commerce of flesh and silver.

SCENE 17
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:40:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Pale [Lvl 5] | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6800/14000 | Gold: 140gp
Conditions: Disguise Self (Active) | Exhaustion: 0]
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20+6 (Advantage) = 21 vs DC 15 - Success (Wisdom (Insight) - Interrogation)

Game Master:
Morning (08:40:00) in the smoldering ruins of the hamlet—you drag the Weaver's Son [Lvl 0] behind the skeletal remains of a soot-blackened chimney, away from the prying eyes and heavy boots of the other men. The boy's breath comes in short, ragged hitches, a sound like a trapped bird beating its wings against a cage. You kneel before him, your magical disguise flickering slightly in the heat rising from the embers, though to his tear-blurred eyes, you are merely another monster in a yellow tabard.

You don't strike him. Instead, you lean in close, the coppery scent of the Riverrun scout's blood still clinging to your gloves. Your violet-tinged eyes, sharp and predatory, lock onto his. (Insight: 21 vs DC 15 - Success) You feel the boy's terror as if it were a physical heat, a delicious vibration in the air. You see the way his eyes dart toward the north end of the village—not toward the elder’s house, but toward the abandoned tannery near the creek. The fear there is different; it's a protective, desperate sort of dread.

"The elder didn't take the silver to his own cellar, did he?" you whisper, your voice a rasping promise of pain. You trail a finger down his cheek, leaving a thin smear of ash and blood. "He put it where the smell of rot would hide the scent of greed. The tannery. Under the soaking vats?"

The boy's sob breaks into a choked gasp, his silence confirming everything your sadistic intuition suspected. "Please," he whimpers, "my father... he's in the pit... please don't..."

You stand, the whimpering boy still at your feet, and glance toward Ser Gregor Clegane [Lvl 15]. The Mountain sits like a statue of iron, watching the smoke rise. You have the location of the communal wealth—a prize far greater than a few silver stags found under a floorboard. 

[SOCIAL SKILL CHALLENGE: Ser Gregor Clegane]
Objective: Join the Mountain's Men
Social HP: 6/8 | Status: RESISTING
Resistance: Currently relegated to 'the dogs' in the muck.

Player (choice: report_to_gregor):

SCENE 18
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:45:00
Location: The Riverlands, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Pale [Lvl 5] | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6800/14000 | Gold: 140gp
Conditions: Disguise Self (Active)]
Resources: HD: 5/5 | Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20 +3 WIS +3 PROF = 22 vs DC 18 - Success (Persuasion (Wisdom-based))

Game Master:
Morning (08:45:00) in the smoldering ruins of the hamlet—you leave the Weaver's Son [Lvl 0] huddled in the ash and stride back toward the center of the track, where the mud has been churned into a dark slurry by the hooves of giant horses. You approach Ser Gregor Clegane [Lvl 15], stopping just outside the reach of his massive shadow. Behind him, the Tickler [Lvl 6] is still busy with his 'work,' the smell of singed hair beginning to drift on the breeze.

"The boy broke," you state, your voice carrying a cold, razor-edged efficiency that cuts through the ambient noise of pillage. You look up at the iron slit of the Mountain's helm, showing no fear, only the calculating hunger of a predator. "The village elder didn't hide their silver in a cellar. He put it in the tannery, submerged in the soaking vats at the creek's edge. He thought the stench of rot would deter common looters."

A thin, cruel smile touches your lips, though it is hidden beneath your magical guise. "The silver is there, untouched. And the elder is likely nearby, watching his wealth drown in lye. I can have the coin and the old man's head at your feet before the fires in the north houses die out."

Ser Gregor Clegane [Lvl 15] shifts in his saddle, the leather groaning under his immense bulk. For a moment, the heavy silence return—a silence where the only sound is the wet, rhythmic sobbing of the survivors. Then, the giant grunts, a sound like grinding stones. "Raff [Lvl 3]! Take the dogs to the tannery. If the silver isn't there, bring me Pale's [Lvl 5] head instead."

He turns his massive horse toward you, the beast's eyes as red and rolling as its master's mood might be. "Go. Bring the coin. And the elder. If you're as fast as you are arrogant, perhaps you'll sleep in a tent tonight instead of the muck."

[SOCIAL SKILL CHALLENGE: Ser Gregor Clegane]
Objective: Join the Mountain's Men
Social HP: 5/8 | Status: WAVERING
Resistance: "If the silver isn't there, bring me Pale's head instead."
Skill Used: Persuasion (Wisdom-based)
Roll Result: 22 vs DC 18
Social HP Damage: 1

Player (choice: ambush_elder):
Scout Ahead - Use your Gloomstalker abilities to slip ahead of Raff [Lvl 3] and ensure the elder doesn&#x27;t flee or sabotage the vats before the men arrive.

SCENE 19
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 AL, Month 1, Day 1, 08:50:00
Location: The Tannery, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Pale [Lvl 5] | HP: 44/44 | XP: 6800/14000 | Gold: 140gp
Conditions: Disguised (Sellsword) | Exhaustion: 0 | Inspiration: No]
Resources: HD: 5/5 | Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20+7 = 9 vs DC 14 - Failure (Stealth (Scouting))
  - 1d20+6 = 22 vs DC 12 - Success (Perception (Locate Elder))

Game Master:
Morning (08:50:00) at the edge of the Mummer's Ford tannery—the air here is a thick, nauseating slurry of sulfur, lye, and the sweet, cloying rot of half-cured hides. You slip away from Raff the Sweet [Lvl 3] and his braying pack of curs, intending to meld into the swirling grey smoke. However, as you move past a stack of drying racks, your boot catches on a heavy wooden frame. The frame clatters against the stone floor with a sharp, echoing crack that seems to ring across the creek. 

"Who's there?" a voice trembles from the shadows of the vat-shed. 

You freeze, your violet eyes narrowing. You have failed to be the ghost you intended, but your senses remain as sharp as a flaying knife. Through the rising steam of the soaking vats, you spot him: the Village Elder [Lvl 0], a man whose skin looks like the very parchment he likely keeps his ledgers on. He is hunched over the third vat, his arms submerged to the elbows in the stinging, milky-white liquid. He is not sabotaging the silver; he is frantically trying to push the iron-bound chests deeper into the sludge, his face a mask of blind, sobbing terror. 

Behind you, you hear the heavy, rhythmic splashing of Raff's [Lvl 3] boots in the mud. They are coming, and the sound of your stumble has already alerted the old man to your presence. He looks up, seeing your disguised, grim visage through the haze. He doesn't run—he can't. His legs seem to have turned to the same lye he's kneeling in. You gain 100 XP for locating the prize, even if the approach was less than silent.

Player (choice: intimidate_elder):
Silence the Elder - Close the distance and use Intimidation to ensure the elder doesn&#x27;t scream or resist when the men arrive.

SCENE 20
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 AL, Month 1, Day 1, 08:52:00
Location: The Tannery, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Pale [Lvl 5] Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 7000/14000 | Gold: 140gp
Conditions: Disguise Self (Active) | Exhaustion: 0 | Inspiration: No]
Resources: HD: 5/5, Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2
Dice Rolls:
  - 1d20 + 2 = 11 vs DC 12 - Failure (Intimidation)

Game Master:
The clatter of the wooden frame still echoes in the damp, sulfurous air as you close the distance. The Village Elder [Lvl 0] jolts, his submerged arms splashing lye onto the stone rim of the vat. He looks up at you, eyes wide and watery, his mouth opening to let out a ragged, thin-blooded shriek of alarm.

You lung forward, your gloved hand clamping over his mouth before the first note of terror can escape. Your other hand catches the front of his threadbare tunic, bunching the wool and hauling him close. Your violet-tinged eyes—unsettling even through the grime of your magical mask—bore into his. 

"Quiet, old man," you hiss, the rasp of your voice cold as a winter grave. "One breath. One sound. And I'll hold your head under that lye until your skin sloughs off like wet paper. Do you understand?"

The Elder [Lvl 0] trembles violently, his muffled gasps hot against your palm. He nods frantically, tears carving clean tracks through the soot on his cheeks. You feel a flicker of that familiar, dark satisfaction as his resistance shatters—he is a broken thing, paralyzed by the weight of your malice. 

Outside, the heavy, rhythmic splashing of Raff the Sweet [Lvl 3] and his men grows louder. They are seconds from the threshold. You keep your hand tight over the Elder's [Lvl 0] face, pinning him against the edge of the vat as the first of the Clegane outriders rounds the corner, their yellow tabards appearing like sickly stains through the shifting steam.

SCENE 22
============================================================
[Timestamp: 298 DR, Month 1 1, 08:52:00
Location: The Tannery, Mummer's Ford Environs
Status: Lvl 5 Ranger | HP: 44/44 | XP: 7000/14000 | Gold: 140gp
Conditions: Disguised (Disguise Self), Sadistic (Advantage on Insight for pain/fear)]
Resources: HD: 5/5 | Spells: L1 3/4, L2 2/2 | Exhaustion: 0
Dice Rolls:

The rejection is as blunt as a mace blow. You gain 0 XP for the failed proposal, and the Social HP for Ser Gregor remains unchanged as he dismisses your strategic insight.

Drive them north toward the forest line before they scatter into the reeds. If we lose even one head to the river, the Mountain will have the difference in your hide. Move!"

Raff [Lvl 3] pauses, his eyes flickering from the cattle to you, then to the massive, iron silhouette of Ser Gregor [Lvl 15]. The memory of the Sept and your display at the ford weighs heavy in the air. He whistles sharply, snapping at Chiswyck [Lvl 3] and the other 'dogs' to begin the drive. "You heard the maester's shadow!" he jeers, though he moves with a rare, panicked efficiency. "Round them up!"

With the outriders occupied, you turn your attention to the fallen Serjeant [Lvl 4]. You perform a final, meticulous audit of his remains, your fingers sliding into the hidden seams of his gambeson. You move to the grain wagons, your fingers dancing over the hidden compartments mentioned in the Elder's ledger. 

Behind a false panel in the second wagon, you find the real prize: a locked coffer containing 150 gold dragons in minted Tully coin and a map detailing the secret river-crossings used by the Blackfish's outriders. The 'dogs' are chasing cows in the mud, but you have claimed the keys to the Riverlands. His massive greatsword clears a literal path through the center of the Tully escort. There is no parrying that slab of steel; it cleaves through a shield, the man holding it, and the ox behind him in a single, sickeningly fluid arc. The screams of the dying are punctuated by the mechanical grinding of Gregor's teeth as he systematic destroys every living thing within his reach. The Red Fork is no longer a river; it is a ledger being balanced in blood.

Late morning (10:25:00) at the Red Fork River-bend—the butchery is nearing its clinical conclusion. From your vantage point on the ridge, the battlefield below is a tapestry of splintered wood, panicked lowing, and the sharp, rhythmic screams of men who have realized no gods are listening. 

You center your breathing, the wood of your longbow cool against your palm. You loose two arrows in rapid succession; the first punches through his thigh, pinning his leg to a submerged root, while the second takes him square between the shoulder blades as he twists. He collapses face-forward into the silt, the Red Fork turning a darker shade of crimson around him.

Below, Raff the Sweet [Lvl 3] intercepts a group of drovers attempting to unhook a team of oxen. He laughs, a high-pitched, boyish sound that carries over the roar of the water, as he caves in a man's chest with a single, crunching blow of his mace. The Tully escort is gone—shattered into meat and memories.

Ser Gregor Clegane [Lvl 15] strides through the center of the carnage, his massive greatsword dripping with thick, dark gore. He doesn't look at the dead; his gaze is fixed on the lead wagon, his heavy gauntlet splintering the wood as he searches for the primary prize. The 'audit' of the Red Fork is complete, leaving only the weighing of the spoils.

You take the girl to a secluded corner of the yard near a trough of cold, brackish water. With the diligence of a man auditing a precious cargo, you begin to clean her. [Perception: 1d20 + 7 = 1 + 7 = 8 vs DC 20 - Critical Failure] But the more you scrub, the more she seems to dissolve into a smudge of grey and brown. You attempt to draw her out with a gentleness that feels like a foreign tongue in your mouth. "The Lion doesn't pick cupbearers for their silence, girl," you rasp, offering her a piece of dried fruit from your rations. "Tell me your name. Where did you learn to hold a gaze like that? Was it in a manor or a hovel?" [Insight: 1d20 + 6 = 6 + 6 = 12 vs DC 15 - Failure] 

The girl takes the fruit with a quick, bird-like motion, but her eyes remain guarded, two hard flints of grey behind a mask of soot. "Arry," she says, her voice a thin, defensive rasp. "I'm just Arry. I learned to look at what's in front of me so I don't trip in the mud." She offers nothing more, her spirit a locked vault that refuses your 'gentle' audit. 

You straighten, the Red Maester's patience wearing thin. She is a 'wild variable' in the Harrenhal ledger, but for now, she is merely a silent, mud-streaked child. You escort her through the scorched halls of the Kingspyre Tower, delivering her to Tywin's personal chambers as ordered. As you leave her at the heavy oak doors, she looks back at you—a single, sharp glance of pure, unadulterated loathing that suggests she hasn't forgotten the 'Red Maester' who broke the women at Mummer's Ford.

You have found the loose thread in her ledger—the unmistakable marks of high birth—but her spirit remains a locked vault. She is not just a variable; she is a survivor who knows exactly what happens to noble girls in the hands of the Mountain's Men.

You've found the crack in the stone.

You offer a slow, thin smile. "Accounting is a trade of necessity, My Lord. Shadows do not pay for themselves, and the North was a ledger that had been left open far too long. I merely provided the ink to close it."

Tyrion chuckles, a sharp bark of amusement. "Ink. I like that. Most men would call it blood, but you... you see the numbers beneath the skin. A dangerous quality in a city where everyone is trying to hide their math."

You turn your gaze toward Sansa Stark [Lvl 1]. She is seated at the edge of the feast, a pale shadow in a room full of golden predators. Her eyes are red-rimmed, her face a mask of practiced, fragile courtesy. You approach her with a deliberate, soft step, dropping the 'Red Maester's' rasp for the silken, courtly lilt you used with Arya. 

"Lady Sansa," you murmur, bowing with a precision that hints at your own noble blood. "The loss of your brother... it is a tragedy that even an auditor's heart can recognize. Robb was a king of high honor, and the realm is a colder place for his absence."

Sansa flinches at the name, her small hands knotting in her lap. She looks up at you, her grey eyes brimming with a terror she is struggling to hide behind her lessons. [Insight: 26 vs DC 15 - Success] You see the raw, jagged grief beneath her skin, but also a flicker of confusion. You are a Lannister Lord, yet you speak of her brother with a reverence that no one else in this room would dare. 

"Robb..." she whispers, her voice a cracked prayer. "They say he was killed by bandits. They say you were there, My Lord."

"I was," you answer, your violet eyes searching hers with a simulated, dark sympathy. "I tried to guide him to the Wall, to a life where he could be safe. The bandits... they were a debt I couldn't audit in time. But you are safe here, My Lady. The Lion is a harsh master, but he protects what is his. If you find the shadows of this tower growing too long, remember that even a ghost knows the way home."

Sansa looks at you, her breath hitching. She doesn't trust you—she shouldn't—but for a heartbeat, she sees a sliver of the North in your words, a ghost of the honor her father died for. You leave her there, a seed of dependence planted in her despair, while the rest of the Council watches the Red Maester move among the high and the low with the same clinical, predatory grace.

He offers his chalice in a silent toast, his eyes locking onto yours with a profound, newfound respect. The alliance is no longer a matter of convenience; it is a shared recognition of intellect in a city of fools.

Tyrion remains silent for a long moment, the wit for once absent from his mismatched eyes. He looks down at his wine, then back at you, a slow nod of understanding pulling at his features. He recognizes the cold, protective logic behind your words.

Often it is absent at the start of these courtly arrangements, yet it has a way of stirring later, once the fear has subsided and the trust has been built. But for the sake of your standing, and for her protection within these walls... the union must be finalized."

Tyrion remains silent for a long moment, the wit for once absent from his mismatched eyes. He looks down at his wine, then back at you, a slow nod of understanding pulling at his features. He recognizes the cold, protective logic behind your words, though the physical reality clearly weighs on him.

Campaign Dossier: Visenya (Silver) Belaerys-Targaryen

**1. Lineage: The Apex Bloodline**
- **House Belaerys:** Your mother was the last of House Belaerys, one of the 'Forty Families' of the Valyrian Freehold and one of the top five dragon-lord lineages. 
- **The Apex Tier:** In the hierarchy of Valyrian blood, Belaerys is considered 'Apex' or 'Original.' Mainline Targaryens are viewed as secondary/trace-descendants by comparison. This purity grants you preternatural beauty and fundamentally stronger magical/dragon affinity.
- **The Betrayal:** While the Targaryens escaped the Doom, the Belaerys line in Westeros was systematically hunted and betrayed by House Peake, who sought to prevent a resurgence of high-Valyrian power.
- **The Secret:** Only the King and his direct heirs (and now the loyalist Vaelon) recognize the true potency of your blood. To the world, you are a boyish squire; to the initiates, you are the Uncrowned Queen.

**2. Future Plot Arcs (Spoiler-Free)**
- **The Peake Retribution (Level 6):** An arc centered on retrieving a lost Belaerys artifact currently held by House Peake. This serves as the 'Dragon Milestone' where your companion's potential is unlocked.
- **The Fourteen Flames Transcendence:** An exploration of the Apotheosis ritual your family was performing that allegedly caused the Doom. You will have to decide if this power is a birthright or a curse.
- **The Remnant War:** A factional struggle between 'Loyalists' (who view you as a future God-Queen and push for your ascension) and 'Doom-Watchers' (antagonists who view your blood as a world-ending threat that must be extinguished).
- **The Simple Path vs. The Golden Throne:** A recurring narrative choice between the humble, honorable life of a knight (inspired by Dunk) and the divine destiny demanded by your Valyrian heritage.

**3. Heritage Injection (For Other Campaigns)**
- **Theme:** 'The Refugee God.' A character who possesses reality-altering potential but chooses to hide in the soot of common life.
- **Mechanical Hook:** The 'Subtle Casting' and 'Eyes of the Dragon' features reflect a being whose magic is so innate it requires no spoken words, and whose wisdom translates directly into physical defense.

Lord Varys [Lvl 10] emerges from the shifting morning mists, his slippers silent against the wet stones. The Spider offers a soft, perfumed smile, but his obsidian eyes are fixed on the broken Mockingbird with a clinical, predatory hunger. He looks at you, his gaze tracing the ethereal lines of your features—a preternatural beauty that seems to glow with the rising sun, a tier of Valyrian perfection that commands a silent, fearful awe from the guarding Gold Cloaks.

"The harvest of the morning is bountiful indeed, Lord Aemon," Varys [Lvl 10] purrs, his voice a silken wheeze. "The mockingbird's wings are clipped, and the threads of his web are fraying in the light. But the city is still restless. My little birds whisper that the Chataya [Lvl 4] has turned her house into a fortress, protecting the secret records Baelish [Lvl 8] left behind."

You lean into Baelish's [Lvl 8] trembling space, your violet eyes burning with a preternatural intensity that makes him flinch as if from a physical blow. You grind your boot into the splintered wood of the wagon, your voice a jagged, low rasp. "The final names, Petyr. The hidden register in the Chataya's rafters. Speak, or I will ensure your silence is the only thing the crows remember of you."

Baelish [Lvl 8] shatters. The last of his cleverness evaporates into a frantic, sobbing submission. [Intimidation: 29 vs DC 15 - Success] "The floorboards... beneath the hearth!" he chokes out, his spirit hollowed by your presence. "The names of the Gold Cloak captains and the debts of the Small Council... it's all there. Spare me... I gave you the truth!"

Varys [Lvl 10] offers a sharp, clinical nod of alignment. "The reckoning of the Street of Silk is at hand. The Lion will be pleased with the clarity you've brought to this morning's chaos."

Game Master:
The ride from the Iron Gate to the Street of Silk is a blur of grey cobblestones and the startled faces of early-rising smallfolk. You ride at the head of the column, your Apex features catching the pale dawn light in a way that makes the commoners draw the sign of the Seven as you pass. Behind you, the covered wagon jolts over the ruts, the rhythmic clink of Petyr Baelish’s chains providing a sharp, metallic underscore to the morning's drizzle. Lord Varys (Lvl 10) rides beside the wagon, his face a mask of serene, lavender-scented contemplation.

As you round the corner into the Street of Silk, the scent of expensive oils is replaced by the sharp, electric tension of a standoff. Ser Maric (Lvl 5) and your elite spears have formed a semi-circle around the entrance to Chataya’s, their shields a wall of Lannister crimson against the heavy, iron-studded doors. Above, on the carved wooden balcony, Chataya (Lvl 4) stands with her arms crossed, her dark eyes flashing with a fierce, protective fire as two of her house-guards level crossbows at the street.

You pull your destrier to a halt, the mud splashing against the polished boots of your guard. You don't offer a greeting. Instead, you signal the men to drag Baelish (Lvl 8) from the back of the wagon. The once-elegant Master of Whispers is a wretched sight, his silk doublet torn and his face a map of mud and dried blood. You catch him by the collar, forcing him to his knees in the center of the street for all to see.

"The Mockingbird has been grounded," you announce, your voice a silken rasp that carries the preternatural weight of your blood. You look up at the balcony, your violet eyes locking onto Chataya’s with a predatory stillness. "The City Watch has already audited his exits, and the Lion has claimed his secrets. Your master has surrendered the register, Chataya. If you wish for your house to remain standing when the sun reaches its zenith, you will open these doors and allow the King's justice to be finalized. If not... the Mountain is only a street away, and his accounting is never peaceful."

The sight of the shackled Baelish—the man who was supposed to be the smartest player in the city—shatters the guards' resolve. Their crossbows dip, and Chataya’s defiant posture crumbles into a look of profound, weary realization. The heavy iron bolts are drawn back, and the doors groan open.

You stride into the opulent, dimly lit interior, leaving the guards to secure the perimeter. You move directly to the massive stone hearth in the primary salon, where a fire still crackles, casting long, dancing shadows against the silk-draped walls. Petyr’s frantic confession rings in your ears: *Beneath the hearth.*

However, as you reach the stones, you find the challenge Baelish failed to mention. The fire is built high, the heat radiating in waves that make your skin prickle. You attempt to use your crowbar to probe the edges of the heavy floorboards, but the soot is thick and the stones are scorching. (Investigation: 6 vs DC 10 - Failure) You find a slight seam, but as you pull, the iron tool slips against the heated masonry, and a cloud of stinging ash erupts, clouding your vision. The secret register is within your reach, but the hearth refuses to yield its prize to a hasty hand.
